# Project Wraith: System Architecture v4.1 (Build-ready)

**Event:** Kiroween 2026 (Devpost), category **Frankenstein** · submission deadline **2026-12-04 14:00 PT** (Saturday 2026-12-05, 05:00 Bangkok time)
**Version:** 4.1.8 · 2026-11-20 · English master copy. The earlier Thai v4.0 is archived outside the repo.
**Related:** `.kiro/steering/` (rules), `.kiro/specs/` (per-unit requirements), `docs/DECISIONS-PENDING.md`, `docs/spikes/RESULTS.md`.
**Primary readers:** the agent loop that builds Wraith phase by phase with Kiro specs (§18–§22), and one human (the owner) who answers only §24.2

> **Confidence labels (same as v3.5):** ✅ confirmed from official docs · 🧪 must be confirmed in a spike · 💡 our own design
> **New abbreviations in v4:** `P<n>` = phase (§18) · `S-<name>` = Kiro spec (§20) · `Q<n>` = spike question (§19) · `SI-<n>` = security invariant that must not be relaxed (§12.1) · `E_<CODE>` = error code (§6.9) · `<phase>-D<n>` = Definition of Done check (§18)

---

## 0. TL;DR

Wraith is **a single chat room that brings together every AI agent ("bot"), whichever machine it runs on or whether it runs in the cloud**. It is used from a phone, a desktop app, and a watch. Every bot lives in a **herdr pane** (a terminal in **herdr-core**, `hc`, DECISION-58), so you can switch between **Chat mode** and **Console mode** at any time.

**No machine is the center.** Every machine (node) and every device (PWA/desktop/watch) connects **outbound** to a **relay**. The relay only forwards, orders, queues, and grants rights. The relay does not run agents and **has no authority to approve on the user's behalf** (everything that enters an agent must carry a device or node signature).

**The relay is a single library (`relay-core`) that runs in 3 modes (§3.2):**

| Mode | Where it runs | Use when |
|---|---|---|
| **Local** (default) | Embedded in `wraith-node` on `127.0.0.1:7777` | One machine. Open `http://localhost:7777` and it works immediately. No accounts needed |
| **Self-hosted** | `wraith-relay` (Node/Docker) on an always-on machine, a VPS, or behind Tailscale | Several machines at home or on a private network |
| **Cloudflare** | Worker + Durable Object in the user's own Cloudflare account | Remote + phone + watch from anywhere, with no open ports |

```
  📱 Phone PWA   💻 Desktop (Tauri)   ⌚ Amazfit (Zepp Side Service, HTTPS)
        │ WSS            │ WSS                │ HTTP API (§6.7)
        ▼                ▼                    ▼
 ┌──────────── relay = relay-core + adapter (Local / Node / Cloudflare) ────────────┐
 │  sequencing · offline queue · presence · leases (fencing) · loop-guard · Web Push │
 └───────▲───────────────────────▲───────────────────────────▲──────────────────────┘
         │ WSS (outbound)         │ WSS (outbound)             │ WSS (outbound)
   node: Mac                node: V100 box              node: (other machines)
   wraith-node ⇄ herdr      wraith-node ⇄ herdr
   ├ pane: sprite → kiro-cli acp       (T1)
   ├ pane: sprite → kiro-cli (cloud)   (T1 Cloud: the machine holding the cloud-host lease)
   ├ pane: sprite → Claude Agent SDK  (T1, also local LLM 🧪; DECISION-47)
   └ pane: any CLI / shell            (T0)
                                   ☁️ Kiro Cloud (cloud-session sandboxes, us-east-1)
```

**5 differentiators + 1 theme feature**
1. **A Kiro Cloud session is a bot** next to the bots running on your machines (in its own chat; cloud bots don't join groups, DECISION-31), and **a cloud bot can hand off work with `@name`** (DECISION-9, the headline feature)
2. **Per-command approval from a phone or watch**, with approvals signed by the device key
3. **Group chat between bots**: they @mention each other, hand off work, and return results to each other
4. **Many vendors in one room**: Kiro (local/cloud), Claude Code, local LLM, and any other CLI
5. **Every terminal is a herdr pane** and can switch to Chat mode
6. **The Crypt:** shows sessions that have closed; press *Resurrect* to bring one back into the room with its original context

**Baseline features** (required, but not selling points): mobile PWA, Web Push, ACP, dashboard, desktop app

### 0.1 Reading map for the agent loop 💡

| If you are about to… | Read |
|---|---|
| Start a new phase | §18 (that phase) → §20 (mapped specs) → §22 (guardrails) |
| Run a spike | §19, the row for that Q + the §-refs in the row |
| Write a schema / parser | §6.1–§6.2, §6.9, §10 |
| Touch anything related to signatures, approval, roster, lease | Always §11–§12 first, and **never relax SI-\*** |
| Build UI | §14 (+ §4.5/§4.6/§4.7) |
| Decide something not covered by this document | §22.1 decision-authority table · If it is in the "ask a human" column, stop, record the question in `docs/DECISIONS-PENDING.md`, and move on to another phase/task that is not blocked |

### 0.2 Changelog v3.5.4 → v4.0 (consistency fixes, not decision changes)

| # | In v3.5.4 | Fixed in v4 |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | Build order in §17 had P4.5/P5.5, and P6 "extensions" lumped unrelated things (Crypt, failover, Capacitor) into one block with no entry/exit criteria | Split into **P0–P9**, each usable on its own, with scope in/out, entry, DoD, deliverables, specs (§18) · The old→new number mapping table is at the end of §18 |
| C2 | sprite had 2 invocation forms: `wraith-sprite --bot … -- kiro-cli acp …` (§4.4) and `--profile architect` (§7.6) | **One standard form:** `wraith-sprite --bot <id> --room <id> --profile <name>`, with argv taken from `profiles.json` (§11.1) · The `-- <argv>` form is allowed only for dev/spike and must not be used in resume argv |
| C3 | `room.freeze` was an unsigned command (§6.2), but §4.7.2 said cap `watch` can do `room.freeze` and "the node checks the cap", even though the relay manages freeze | `room.freeze` is a **signed command**, and **the relay checks the cap/signature of commands the relay handles itself** (freeze, crypt.list, console.open). The node checks what reaches the node (§6.2, §12) |
| C4 | The watch Side Service "verifies the node signature on the request", but there was no way for the watch to get the roster | Added `GET /api/v1/roster` (owner-signed), and the watch pins the owner public key at pairing, like a node (§6.7) |
| C5 | §4.7.2 said a button in a Zepp notification "can open a Mini Program page or call the App Service" | Official docs ✅ state that a `notify()` action has only `{text, file, param}`, which **starts an App Service file** (single execution, limited to **600 ms**, no timers) → the Allow button is designed so the App Service sends a message to the Side Service within 600 ms 🧪 **Q27b**, with a fallback that opens the app from the notification center (§4.7.2, §14.4) |
| C6 | `crypt.list` was specified as D→R→N even though `crypt-index` lives in the relay | `crypt.list` is D→R (the relay answers from the index). `crypt.resurrect` stays D→R→N and must be signed (§6.2) |
| C7 | `console.input` used the counter `n` (§4.3) and `ts` within 60s (§8), scattered across different places | Conditions consolidated in one place: there must be an unexpired take-control session + `n` increasing + `ts` within 60s (§6.2, §8) |
| C8 | `requestHash` was defined as "SHA-256 of the canonical `approval.request` the user sees", unclear canonical of what | Defined as the same bytes as the signature scope: `SHA-256(JCS({v, meta, body}))` of the `approval.request` envelope (§6.1) |
| C9 | Q24 combined 2 unrelated questions (WebCrypto in WKWebView, and `bun --compile` + native module) | Split into Q24a/Q24b, Q27 split into Q27a/Q27b, Q25 gained Q25b (`fetch` timeout for long-poll) (§19) |
| C10 | The `.kiro/specs` list in §15 had 11 entries, missing protocol/crypto/pwa/desktop/watch/demo/ops | Full list of 24 specs with EARS requirements, dependencies, and phase (§20) |
| C11 | Error codes were scattered across §6.2, §7.4, §12 | A single `E_*` catalogue with origin, HTTP status, and required client action (§6.9) |
| C12 | No contract for the adapter interface, config file, CLI, HTTP API, migrations | Added §6.7, §6.8, §10.3, §11 |
| C13 | The device `hello` (§6.2) and the node `hello` (§6.3) had different shapes without saying so | A single `hello` with discriminator `kind: "device"\|"node"` (§6.2) |
| C14 | §4.6 said the sidecar is built with `bun build --compile` or Node SEA 🧪, but `better-sqlite3` is a native module | Default set to **Node SEA + `better-sqlite3` prebuilt**, with Q24b choosing (bun if it passes) · Last-resort fallback: sidecar = portable Node runtime + JS bundle (§4.6) |
| C15 | The TL;DR diagram had no watch | Added the watch to topology and membership (§3.1, §5.1) |

---

## 1. Goals / Non-goals

### Goals
- **G1:** Send a message to one or more bots and see streaming replies on the phone
- **G2:** Approve or reject a bot action from the phone within 2 taps, with **Web Push** when a bot is blocked (iOS requires installing the PWA to the Home Screen and iOS 16.4 or later ✅) · If Q19 fails, fall back to in-app notifications (DECISION-8)
- **G3:** See the Console of every bot through its herdr pane (in Wraith's Console, or with `hc attach` / Herdr.app at the machine)
- **G4:** Bots can hand off work to each other, results are returned to the requester, and there is loop protection
- **G5:** Bring bots from several machines and from Kiro Cloud into one room **with no machine as a single point of failure** · **Accepted trade-off:** the relay is still a single point of failure for approvals, because approval from the pane is disabled (DECISION-2). If the relay is unavailable, an approval waits until timeout and is then rejected (safe, but work stops) · **Mitigated from P5:** at the machine itself, the desktop app approves over its direct local channel to the node (DECISION-22); remote devices still need the relay
- **G6:** Bring closed sessions back (The Crypt)
- **G7:** A Demo/Replay site that judges can open until 2027-01-29, with no login, no credits used, and no connection to real machines
- **G8:** **Usable standalone immediately** (Local mode), and **remote/mobile can be enabled by the user** with a relay in their own Cloudflare account or on their own machine
- **G9 (new):** Approve `allow_once`/`reject_once` and view bot status from an **Amazfit Active 2 (Square)**, with the watch holding limited rights (cap `watch`)
- **G10 (new):** A desktop app (Tauri) that works immediately in Local mode when opened, with a tray badge and native notifications
- **G11 (new):** **One folder, one bot.** Opening a workspace folder always returns the same bot (same session, same history), and the folder tree is the bot hierarchy (DECISION-24)

### Non-goals
- No multi-user support and no RBAC (one Space = one owner, many devices)
- Do not fork herdr-core (send missing pieces upstream as an issue or PR)
- **Do not parse TUIs to build chat bubbles** (T0 can show a "raw snapshot", clearly labelled)
- Do not build terminals outside herdr
- **No public hosted relay** (DECISION-7) · **No E2E** (DECISION-6)
- No relay-less P2P/CRDT
- No native mobile app before Capacitor (P9) · No Apple Watch before there is an iOS app (P9)
- **No native Windows before P9.21** (DECISION-59): macOS and Linux are supported; Windows users run the node inside WSL2 from P9.20 (§4.10)

---

## 2. Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Space** | One user's area, containing all rooms, machines, devices, and bots. It has one relay instance (`spaceId`, random 128 bits) |
| **Room** | A chat. Two kinds (DECISION-28): a **bot chat** (`dm-<botId>`, exactly one per bot, created with the bot, you + that bot) and a **group** (created by the user, members = existing bots the user picks; a bot can be in many groups). `meta.room` is always the chat a prompt came from |
| **relay-core** | All relay logic (sequencer, queue, presence, lease, loop-guard, router, roster, pairing, approvals meta, push, crypt-index, HTTP API), written in pure TypeScript. It uses the platform through the `Storage`, `Sockets`, `Timers`, `Http` interfaces (§6.8) |
| **Relay adapter** | What runs relay-core on a real platform: the **Node adapter** (Local mode embedded in wraith-node, and Self-hosted mode as `wraith-relay`) and the **Cloudflare adapter** (Worker + Space DO) · There is an **in-memory adapter** for tests |
| **herdr** | In this document: **herdr-core** (`hc` ≥ 0.1.18, Apache-2.0) ✅, the terminal runtime under Herdr.app; programs live in holder processes, so updating or restarting the core leaves them running ✅ and, from 0.1.17, so does quitting Herdr.app ✅ (CHANGELOG 0.1.17). Not the older **herdr TUI**, whose server is a different program that does not share terminals with herdr-core ✅ (DECISION-58) |
| **Pane** | One herdr-core terminal that Wraith started (Wraith keeps the word *pane* so it never clashes with Terminal mode, §8.1). It carries labels `wraith.node`, `wraith.bot`, `wraith.session` (§7.6) |
| **Node** | A machine with herdr-core + `wraith-node`. All nodes are **equal** |
| **Device** | A paired user browser/PWA/desktop WebView/watch. It has an ECDSA P-256 key pair (PWA: WebCrypto non-extractable · watch: key in Side Service storage 🧪 Q26) |
| **Owner key** | The key of the first device, which created the Space. It is the **root of trust** and **the only key that can sign the roster** |
| **Roster** | The member list of the Space (`device`/`node` + signing key + caps + hostPriority). Every entry is signed by the owner key. It has a `rosterVersion` that always increases |
| **Cap** | A roster member's rights: `owner`, `device` (Full), `watch` (Approve only), `viewer` (View only), `node`, `cloud-host` · Device caps are chosen by the owner at pairing · The rights table is in §12.2 |
| **Signed command** | Everything that feeds an agent or commands the system must carry a signature (full list in §6.2, column "Signed") · **So the relay can never feed work into an agent by itself** |
| **Workspace** | A folder on one node (`<nodeId>:<realpath>`, e.g. `macbook-pro:~/code/app`) or, for a cloud bot, a GitHub repo + branch. The unit a human thinks in. **Exactly one bot per workspace** (DECISION-24) |
| **Bot** | The single agent that owns one workspace; its main session and each group seat run in their own herdr pane (`seats:"process"`; in `swap` mode all its sessions share the bot's one pane and process, DECISION-54). Its `botId` is a ULID created at first spawn and stored with the workspace, so reopening the folder, resuming or resurrecting always gives the same bot, and a Move keeps it. Its **name** (unique, used for `@mention`) is separate from its identity (DECISION-27) |
| **Parent bot** | The bot whose workspace is the nearest ancestor folder (same node) of another bot's workspace. It leads its child bots and may also write below its own folder; other bots write only in their own workspace (DECISION-25) |
| **Pane ref** | `nodeId/paneId`, where `paneId` is the hc address (`t7`), unique forever on one core but not across machines ✅ |
| **Tier** | T0 Generic / T1 ACP / T2 Native (§5) |
| **Sprite** | `wraith-sprite`, the per-bot process that runs in a pane and drives its agent: as an ACP client (`--mode acp`), through the Claude Agent SDK (`--mode sdk`), as one-shot runs (`--mode exec`), and in Terminal mode hands its pane to the agent's own TUI (§8.1) |
| **Sprite epoch** | A counter that increases every time the sprite or the agent under it is (re)started. Used to prevent id collisions |
| **Agent** | Which CLI a bot runs: `kiro`, `claude`, `local-llm`, `shell` (or `kiro-cloud`). Launch config (argv, tier, safety) is **managed by Wraith** in `~/.wraith/agents.json`; only agents detected on that machine are offered (§11.1, DECISION-26) |
| **Instructions** | Optional free text per bot (persona/role, like a CLAUDE.md for that bot). Empty by default, editable any time. Project instructions still come from the workspace itself (`.kiro/steering/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) |
| **Session ref** | `{agentKind, sessionId, cwd, nodeId, location: local\|cloud}` |
| **Lease** | The right to hold one session, granted by the relay to one holder at a time, with a **fencing token** (`leaseEpoch`) (§7.7) |
| **Cloud-host lease** | A special lease for a cloud bot. It says which node is running the cloud bot's sprite (§7.4.2) |
| **Crypt** | The registry of sessions with no running pane and no lease that can still be resumed |
| **Meta / Body / Relay fields** | The envelope is split into `meta` (route), `body` (content), and `relay` (fields the relay adds, outside the signature) (§6.1) |
| **Spike** | A short experiment that answers one Q. Its result must be recorded in `docs/spikes/RESULTS.md` before any dependent phase starts (§19) |
| **Fake** | Test doubles: `fake-acp-agent`, `fake-hc`, `fake-kiro-exec`, `fake-push-service`, `fake-tui` (§21.2) |

---
## 3. System Context & Deployment

### 3.1 Topology (diagram = Cloudflare mode, the hardest case)

```
┌──────────────────────────────── Internet ─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  📱 iPhone (PWA)   💻 Desktop (Tauri/PWA)   ⌚ Amazfit ⇄ BLE ⇄ Zepp app (iOS) Side Service │
│                                              🎬 Judges → Demo site (static)        │
└────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
         │ WSS + signed cmds│                         │ HTTPS /api/v1/* (signed, §6.7)
┌────────▼─────────────────▼─────────────────────────▼── Cloudflare (user's own account) ─┐
│ Worker  wraith-relay                                                                 │
│   ├─ static assets: PWA build (same origin as WS)                                    │
│   ├─ /ws?space=<id>  → challenge-signature → forward to Space DO                     │
│   ├─ /api/v1/*       → verify request signature → Space DO (HTTP API for watch)      │
│   └─ secrets: SETUP_TOKEN, VAPID private key                                         │
│ Space DO (SQLite, WebSocket Hibernation ✅)                                           │
│   ├─ roster · rooms · bots · prompts(meta) · approvals(meta) · events                 │
│   ├─ sequencer (global seq) · offline queue · presence                                │
│   ├─ lease authority (fencing) · cloud-host scheduler · loop-guard (meta)             │
│   └─ push (Web Push/VAPID) · single multiplexed alarm (lease, old-event cleanup)      │
└───────▲──────────────────────────▲──────────────────────────▲───────────────────────┘
        │ WSS outbound              │ WSS outbound              │
┌───────┴──────── Node: Mac ───┐ ┌─┴────── Node: V100 box ───┐ ┌┴── Node: … ──┐
│ wraith-node                  │ │ wraith-node               │ │              │
│  ├ relay-link (WSS + outbox) │ │  (same codebase)          │ │              │
│  ├ herdr-client · sprite-hub  │ │                           │ │              │
│  ├ mcp-endpoint · hook-endpt │ │  + litellm/vllm (P9)      │ │              │
│  ├ verifier (roster, sigs)   │ │                           │ │              │
│  ├ crypt-scanner · console   │ │                           │ │              │
│  └ local SQLite (~/.wraith)  │ │                           │ │              │
│ herdr-core ── panes          │ │ herdr-core ── panes       │ │              │
└──────────────────────────────┘ └───────────────────────────┘ └──────────────┘
                 ☁️ Kiro Cloud (models + cloud-session sandboxes, us-east-1)
```

- **No inbound ports, no tunnel, no own domain:** every node and every device connects outbound to `<name>.workers.dev` (or the user's domain, if they have one)
- **Works on one machine:** one node + a phone covers every feature
- **One DO per Space, not per room:** leases (one per agent session: main and every seat) must be unique across rooms, the roster is shared, and one DO easily handles a single user 💡

### 3.2 Relay run modes 💡

```
Local mode (default)                        Self-hosted mode                      Cloudflare mode
┌─────────── single machine ─────────┐       ┌─ any machine / VPS ────┐             ┌─ Cloudflare (user acct) ───┐
│ 💻 browser → http://localhost:7777 │       │ wraith-relay (Node)    │             │ Worker + Space DO          │
│ wraith-node                        │       │  = relay-core + Node   │             │  = relay-core + CF adapter │
│  ├ relay-core + Node adapter (emb.)│       │    adapter             │             └──▲──────────▲──────▲───────┘
│  └ herdr ⇄ sprites                  │       └──▲──────────▲──────────┘                │          │      │
└────────────────────────────────────┘          │ WS(S)    │ WS(S)                   node ...   📱/💻   ⌚
                                             node ...   📱/💻/⌚ (HTTPS required)
```

| | Local | Self-hosted | Cloudflare |
|---|---|---|---|
| External account/service required | No | No (except Tailscale, if chosen) | Cloudflare account (Free plan works ✅) |
| Multiple machines | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Phone / watch | ❌ (localhost only) | ✅ with HTTPS (`tailscale serve`, domain + Caddy) | ✅ immediately |
| Use from outside home | ❌ | Depends on network | ✅ no open ports |
| Machine running the relay is off | Everything is on one machine anyway | Room stops forwarding (bots keep working in local-only) | No problem |
| Liveness / quota saving | Plain ping + in-memory state | Same as Local | Hibernation + auto-response (§3.6) |

- **Why the phone needs HTTPS:** WebCrypto, service workers, and PWA install on iOS work only in a secure context (`https://` or `localhost`) · The Zepp Side Service cannot reach the Mac's `localhost`
- **Modes can be migrated:** start in Local, then upgrade with `wraith-node relay migrate --to <url>`, which sends the roster and room history to the new relay (P8) · If history is not migrated, you can create a new Space and pair again (available from P4)
- **Accepted trade-off:** multiple machines without Cloudflare need one machine to run `wraith-relay`. It is a process separate from the node and can be moved anywhere. It does not make that machine more important than others as a bot host

### 3.3 Process inventory

| Process | Where | Role | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|
| relay (`relay-core` + adapter) | Embedded in `wraith-node` (Local) · `wraith-relay` (Self-hosted) · Cloudflare (Worker + Space DO) | Forwarding, ordering, queueing, lease authority, Web Push, HTTP API, serving the PWA | P1 / P4 / P4 |
| `wraith-node` | Every node | Talks to the local herdr, accepts sprites, serves MCP/hook on localhost, verifies command signatures, keeps the outbox | P1 |
| `wraith-sprite` | In a herdr pane (1 per agent session: main + each group seat) | ACP client, renderer, owner of the prompt queue, ring buffer, reports state to herdr with OSC 7501 | P1 |
| herdr-core (`hc`) | Every node | Pane runtime (holders keep programs alive through core restarts and updates), OSC 7501 status, JSON-RPC socket API ✅ (DECISION-58) | – (external) |
| `kiro-cli` | Under the sprite | Primary agent ✅ | – (external) |
| Claude Code (driven through the **Claude Agent SDK**, sprite `--mode sdk`, DECISION-47) | Under the sprite | Secondary bot, also used with local LLM | P9 |
| `litellm` / `vllm` | V100 box | Local inference | P9 |
| Wraith.app (Tauri) | Desktop | WebView + tray + `wraith-node` sidecar | P5 |
| Zepp Mini Program | Watch + Zepp app on iPhone | Device App + Side Service + Settings App | P7 |
| launchd / systemd units | Every node | Start `wraith-node` after login/reboot; the node starts herdr-core with `hc server start` if its socket is missing 🧪 (Q14) | P1 (script) / P8 (reboot test) |

### 3.4 Installation and pairing 💡

0. **Local mode:** run `wraith-node start` for the first time → the node creates a built-in Space and prints the link `http://localhost:7777/setup#<SETUP_TOKEN>` → open it in a browser on the same machine, then do step 2 · A node on the same machine pairs itself automatically (the owner approves once on the setup page; no QR scan is needed because the node and relay are in the same process)
1. **Deploy the relay (Cloudflare / Self-hosted):** the "Deploy to Cloudflare" button, `pnpm --filter @wraith/relay-cf deploy`, or `docker run wraith-relay` · `wraith-relay init` creates the VAPID key and the **`SETUP_TOKEN`** (random 128 bits) as secrets, then shows the setup link once
2. **Create the Space:** open the setup link on the phone → the phone creates the **owner key** → `POST /space` requires a valid `SETUP_TOKEN` → the relay stores the owner public key · A **recovery code** (base32, 26 characters ≈ 130 bits) is shown once · The relay stores only its Argon2id hash · **iOS Safari guard (same as item 4):** on iOS Safari (not `display-mode: standalone`) `/setup` does **not** create the owner key; it shows "Add Wraith to your Home Screen first" + **Copy setup link**, and the installed PWA's `/setup` accepts the pasted link/token. The setup token is used up only when `POST /space` succeeds, so opening the link in Safari first is harmless
3. **Add a node (two-way verification with no typing):**
   - The owner (phone) taps "Add machine" → creates a **pairing ticket** at the relay (`ticketId`, valid 10 minutes, single use) and shows the `ticketId`
   - **Ticket lifetime:** a ticket is consumed when the relay **binds a `pair.offer` to it** (not when a page or link containing it is opened), so opening a pairing link twice is harmless; one ticket binds exactly **one offer of 1–2 members** (2 only for Wraith.app Join, below) and expires 10 minutes after creation
   - Run `wraith-node pair <relay-url> <ticketId>` → the node uses its `node.key` (created only if absent, item 7) + a **random 128-bit secret** and shows a QR = `{nodeSignPub, secret}` · The node connects to the relay via `/pair?ticket=…`
   - The phone scans the QR **directly** (the secret does not pass through the relay) → the owner builds the node's roster entry **only from the QR content** (`signPub`, `kind`, requested `caps`; never from the relay's `pair.offer`), signs it, and sends `pair.complete{ownerPub, rosterEntries, hmac}` through the relay, where `hmac = HMAC-SHA-256(secret, ownerPub‖spaceId‖rosterVersion‖SHA-256(JCS(rosterEntries sorted by memberId)))` (one entry for a single member, two for Join) · **byte framing (C78):** `ownerPub` = 33-byte compressed SEC1 P-256 point · `spaceId` = UTF-8 bytes · `rosterVersion` = 8-byte big-endian unsigned integer · the digest = 32 raw bytes; concatenated in that order (only `spaceId` has variable length, so fields cannot shift) · `rosterEntries` sorted by ascending UTF-16 code units of `memberId` · the tag travels as base64url and is compared in constant time · the secret is 16 bytes from `crypto.getRandomValues` via `packages/crypto`
   - **Pairing payload (one format for every member: node, browser, app, phone, watch):** the QR and the text **pairing code** carry the same bytes: `WRP1-` + base32 (RFC 4648, no padding, shown in groups of 4) of `kind (1 B: node / device / watch / node+device) ‖ signPub (compressed P-256, 33 B) ‖ second signPub (33 B, node+device only) ‖ secret (16 B) ‖ CRC-16` → about 84 characters (136 for Join). The owner takes **every** `signPub` from this payload; `pair.offer` is used only to find the pending pairing for the ticket, and an offer whose keys differ from the payload aborts pairing (`E_PAIR_HMAC_MISMATCH`). There is **no short fingerprint-only code**: a short fingerprint could be matched by a key ground by a malicious relay. The **key fingerprint** shown on both screens (`SHA-256(signPub)` over the 33-byte compressed point (C78), first 8 bytes as 4 groups) is only for the human to compare; it is never used to verify
   - The node checks that the HMAC matches **and** that its own entry in `rosterEntries` has its own key as `signPub` and the caps it asked for. A relay that swaps keys or escalates caps makes pairing fail (`E_PAIR_HMAC_MISMATCH`)
   - The node verifies the HMAC. Only if it passes does it **pin the owner public key** in `~/.wraith/spaces/<spaceId>/trust.json` (item 7)
   - **UX details (DECISION-36):** the Add machine screen shows the whole command with a **Copy** button (`wraith-node pair <relay-url> <ticketId>`) and a 10-minute countdown, so nothing is typed by hand · The node prints the QR **and** the same payload as a text **pairing code** (`WRP1-…`). The owner device either scans the QR (phone) or pastes the code (`/pair/scan` → "Paste code instead"); a **desktop owner** (no camera) uses the code. The code carries the secret, so it must travel by the user's own hands (screen → clipboard/eyes), never through Wraith; the UI says so · If the new machine runs **Wraith.app**, it asks for the relay URL + ticket in a window and shows the QR/code there (no terminal) · On a headless machine over SSH the terminal QR works; `--code-only` prints just the code · The confirm screen shows the machine name (editable), OS, herdr version, requested caps and a short **key fingerprint** that the terminal also prints, so the owner can compare them before tapping Pair
   - **Local mode cannot add machines (a UI rule caused by reachability, DECISION-36/43):** the Local relay listens on `127.0.0.1` of the first machine, so other machines and the phone cannot reach it. In Local mode, Add machine opens "Needs an external relay" with a button to **Settings → Relay** (Cloudflare / self-hosted). Switching creates **or joins** a Space on that relay (item 7: Create on my phone / Create here / Join), and the machine joins it with `wraith-node pair` like any other; the old local Space's data stays on disk (`~/.wraith/relay-local/`) as an archive that is not served while the machine uses the external relay, and its history is not moved before P8 (`relay migrate`)
   - **relay-core has no mode-specific pairing rule:** any member that completes pairing is accepted, whatever the mode. So a **second `wraith-node` on the same machine** can pair with the Local relay through `127.0.0.1` (P2 uses this for its two-node tests with `pair --dev --print-code`); no dev switch exists or is needed, because pairing still requires the owner's signature and the out-of-band secret
4. **Add other devices** (desktop browser, Tauri, watch): same procedure. The new device shows a QR + text pairing code in the same `WRP1-` format (the watch shows only the code, §4.7.2) → **only the owner device** scans or pastes it and signs the roster entry
   - **Add device UX (DECISION-37):** the owner taps **Add device** → a ticket is shown as a **pairing link** `https://<relay>/pair#<ticketId>` plus its QR (the ticket is not secret; the phone camera can open it) → the new device opens the link (or opens `/pair` and pastes the ticket) → it creates its key + secret and shows **QR + text pairing code + key fingerprint**, waiting → the owner scans or pastes → the confirm screen shows the device kind/browser and the owner **chooses its cap**: **Full** (`device`), **Approve only** (`watch` cap, also usable for a phone), or **View only** (`viewer`, new) → Pair. Unlike a machine, a device never chooses its own caps; the owner's choice goes into the signed roster entry and the HMAC. `caps` in a device's `pair.offer` or `POST /api/v1/pair` is an advisory hint that the owner device ignores (a `watch`-kind code is always paired as `[watch]`)
   - An **unpaired browser** that opens the relay sees only `/pair`; with no ticket it says "Ask your owner device for a pairing link". There is no password login
   - **iOS Safari guard:** on iOS, Safari and the Home Screen PWA have **separate storage**, and Web Push works only in the Home Screen PWA. So when `/pair` detects iOS Safari (not `display-mode: standalone`) it **does not create a key**; it shows "Add Wraith to your Home Screen first" (Share → Add to Home Screen → open Wraith from the Home Screen) and a **Copy ticket** button. The installed PWA's `/pair` then asks for the ticket (paste) and continues normally; this works because opening the link did not consume the ticket. The same guard protects `/setup` (item 2). Android/desktop browsers pair in place (installing is optional)
   - **Wraith.app "Join my Space"** has an **"Also run bots on this Mac"** switch (DECISION-38; default **on** when `doctor` finds herdr + an agent, otherwise **off**). On: both roles in one confirmation, the node (machine) and the app window (device); both public keys travel in one QR/code (kind node+device), the owner sees "mac-mini · machine + desktop app" and signs both roster entries at once (one HMAC over both entries). Off: device only; the bundled `wraith-node` is **not started** and has no key
   - **Join protocol and secret custody (DECISION-42):** (1) the app window creates its device key (WebCrypto, non-extractable) and hands its public key to Rust; (2) Rust spawns `wraith-node pair <relay> <ticket> --json --device-pub <jwk>` (or `wraith-node pair … --json` for device-only, where the node creates no node key and only relays the pairing); (3) the **node generates the 128-bit secret** and its own key, sends `pair.offer{members:[node, device]}` to the relay, and writes `{code, qrPayload, secret, fingerprints}` as JSON to its **stdout**; (4) Rust reads it from the child's stdout pipe and passes it to the window over Tauri's in-process IPC; the window shows QR + code + fingerprints; (5) after the owner signs, the node receives `pair.complete`, verifies the HMAC and **its own entry** (signPub = its key, requested caps) and prints the result on stdout; the window verifies **its own entry** (signPub = its key, `kind:"device"`) with the same secret, then pins `ownerPub`. The `--json` stdout is never written to any log (SI-12). The secret and code never travel over the relay, `/local/v1/ws`, a localhost HTTP endpoint, or any channel except that parent-child pipe and the user's own screen
   - **Machine vs device (DECISION-38):** a computer is a **machine** only while its `wraith-node` runs with a node key in the roster (it hosts bots); it is a **device** when something on it holds a device key to sign commands (browser, PWA, app window, watch). Installing Wraith.app ships the node binary but does not make the computer a machine. **Upgrade:** Wraith.app → Machines & devices → **Run bots on this Mac** → `doctor` (herdr + agent, with install hints) → start the node → the same Add machine confirmation on the owner device (QR/code, no terminal). **Downgrade:** the owner revokes only the machine role. When a node sees a verified roster that revokes its own key, it stops all its sprites (sessions go to the Crypt), deletes `node.key` (the Space folder `spaces/<spaceId>/` and `node.json relay` stay, so a later **Run bots on this Mac** re-pairs into the same Space), and exits; its bots show `offline`; the app window's device key is untouched and keeps working · **Pause** (Settings → This Mac → **Run bots on this Mac** off, remote mode) is different: the node sends node-signed `node.paused{paused:true}`, stops its sprites and disconnects; bots show as `paused`, the roster entry stays, and turning it back on sends `node.paused{paused:false}` with its next `hello` and resumes without pairing · **In Local mode** the node is also the relay, so stopping it would disconnect everything: the switch is replaced by **Pause all bots** (sprites stop, bots show `paused`, node and relay keep running) (DECISION-43)
   - **Watch:** the code is shown in the Zepp app's Settings page (§4.7.2); the watch itself shows "Finish pairing on your phone". The owner pastes the code into Add device (cap preset to Approve only)
5. **Remove a device or node:** the owner signs a `revoke` entry → the relay drops the connection, and every node stops accepting signatures from that key (permanently)
6. **Owner device lost (P8):** use the recovery code on a new device to create a new owner key → **every node runs `wraith-node trust --rotate`** (new QR + secret) → the new owner signs a whole new roster · Until rotation, nodes still trust the old owner key (the relay rejecting connections from the old key is the temporary barrier)

7. **Switching the relay (C70/C71, DECISION-56):** the three Settings → Relay cards (**Local**, **Your Cloudflare**, **Self-hosted**) are **actionable only in Wraith.app on a machine** (a running bundled or connected `wraith-node`); everywhere else (phone, browser, a device-only Wraith.app, the watch) they are read-only with "Change the relay on a machine: Wraith.app or `wraith-node`" (`E_RELAY_SWITCH_LOCAL_ONLY`). There is **no protocol message, HTTP endpoint or `/local/v1/ws` call** that changes a node's relay: the app drives its own node through the Rust parent process (argv + stdout pipe, the Join custody of DECISION-42); headless machines use the CLI. Reason: a remote command that re-points a node would let a stolen device move every bot to a relay it controls. **Phases:** the CLI path is P4 (`wraith-node pair <url> <ticket>` from Local, `wraith-node unpair` back, P4-D0); the wizard is P5 (P5-D13).
   - **Per-Space storage (C71):** a machine can have belonged to several Spaces but is connected to **one** at a time. Each Space has its own folder `~/.wraith/spaces/<spaceId>/` with `trust.json` (owner pin, roster, revoked list); `node.json relay = {mode, url, spaceId}` selects the active one; switching never deletes another Space's folder. **`node.key` is one per machine** and every `pair` reuses it (created only when absent; after a DECISION-38 downgrade deleted it, the next pair creates a new one). `node.db` rows that belong to a Space carry `space_id` (§10.2). The Local relay's own data stays in `~/.wraith/relay-local/`. The app window keeps **one device key per Space** (IndexedDB keyed by `spaceId`, §4.5); `/local/v1/ws` uses the active Space's key.
   - **Wizard "Use an external relay" (Your Cloudflare / Self-hosted, P5):** (a) **Relay URL** (`https://` required; `http://` only for `127.0.0.1` and `*.ts.net`) with help: Cloudflare = **Deploy to Cloudflare** + "copy the setup link it shows once"; Self-hosted = `docker run … wraith-relay` + **Copy**; the WS URL is derived as `wss://<host>/ws` · (b) **Check** = `GET <url>/healthz`: no answer, TLS error, 10 s timeout or a body that is not a Wraith relay → `E_RELAY_UNREACHABLE`; a Wraith relay with another contract `version` → `E_UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` · (c) `spaceCreated:false` → **"Who holds the owner key?"** (the owner key adds and revokes machines and devices; it does not run bots or the relay): **Create on my phone** (default, recommended for anyone with a phone or watch): paste the setup link the relay printed at deploy → the Mac shows it as a QR + **Copy** (it contains the setup token: shown only on this screen, never logged, SI-12) → the phone opens it (iOS Home Screen guard, item 2) → creates the Space and becomes owner (recovery code on the phone) → on the phone **Add machine** → the wizard continues with **Join** · **Create here (this Mac becomes owner)**: paste the setup link → the app window creates the new Space's owner key and calls `POST /space` → recovery code (must be confirmed as saved); this writes nothing on the node; in that Space the window's device key **is** the owner key (no `--device-pub`), stored in the Mac's IndexedDB keyed by the relay URL until the Space id is known (clearing app storage then means the recovery code, item 6); if the wizard is cancelled afterwards the window keeps that key and a later run with the same URL goes straight to Switch · `spaceCreated:true` → **Join**: paste the ticket from Add machine on the owner device · (d) **Switch** (the only step that changes the machine) — allowed only when **every bot on this Mac is `idle`, `paused`, `dormant`, `closed` or `failed`**, no `prompts_local` row of this node is `queued`/`sent_to_sprite`/`running`, no `terminal_stays` row is open and no cloud-host lease is held; otherwise the node answers `busy{reasons[]}` over `control.sock` and the wizard lists them ("@app is working · 2 queued messages · @app-cloud is hosted here"); the switch itself turns **Autopilot off** for every bot (a §7.2.1 off transition) and **clears `grants`** (Allow-always given by devices of the old Space): **Join** = `wraith-node pair <url> <ticket> --json --device-pub <jwk>` → QR + `WRP1-` code (node+device, item 4) → the owner pairs on their device; **Create here** = the window (owner) issues a ticket itself, runs `wraith-node pair <url> <ticket> --json` (node only), builds the node's `RosterEntry` from the pipe payload and signs `roster.update` + `pair.complete` with the HMAC over the pipe secret (DECISION-42 custody, SI-11); in both cases the node verifies, writes `spaces/<spaceId>/trust.json`, sets `node.json relay`, reconnects. Confirm text before Switch: "This Mac leaves its Local Space. Bots keep running. Local chats stay on this Mac and are not moved before P8. Phones and other machines pair with the new relay."
   - **What a switch does on the node (both directions):** `outbox` rows of the old Space stay unsent (sent if the machine returns to that Space, dropped after 7 days like `outbox_relay`) · `hello{lastSeq, lastAckedId}` use the new Space's own values (0 / none the first time) · every bot is re-announced with `bot.registered` (the relay creates missing `dm-<botId>` rooms); rooms whose bot this node no longer has are shown read-only "bot gone" · group seats of the old Space are closed (rows kept as `state='closed'` with their `space_id`, resumable if the machine returns) · cloud-bot leases are re-acquired in the new Space · sprites, herdr panes, main sessions, `workspaces` and the Crypt are untouched (they belong to the machine, not the Space).
   - **Local (while on an external relay) → "Go back to Local"** (also refused while work is running): confirm "Phones, the watch and other machines can no longer reach this Mac. Bots on this Mac move back to the Local Space; its chats come back." → `wraith-node unpair` = `node.json relay = {mode:"local", spaceId:<local>}`; the external Space's folder is kept and this Mac shows offline there until the owner revokes it · the node serves the Local Space again and the window uses its Local key · if the Local roster does not contain the current `node.key` (it was recreated), the node pairs itself again as in item 0 (the owner approves once on `/setup`) · with no Local Space at all, a new one is created as in item 0.
   - **Current relay row:** URL, mode, online state and the **owner key fingerprint** (read-only). Moving the same Space to another URL is `relay migrate` (P8), not this wizard.

8. **A machine without the app (headless, C76, DECISION-59):** for a Mac mini, a Linux GPU box or a server that runs bots and has no screen. Nothing is driven from it; the owner uses the phone, a browser or Wraith.app on another computer.
   1. Install the node: `brew install <owner>/wraith/wraith-node` (macOS, or Linux with Homebrew) or unpack the release tarball (§4.6.1) and put `bin/` on PATH
   2. Install herdr-core with its official installer and at least one agent CLI (`kiro-cli`); `wraith-node init`, then `wraith-node doctor` until it passes
   3. Join a Space: this needs an **external relay** (Local mode listens on `127.0.0.1` of this machine only, item 3). On the owner device: **Add machine** → copy the command → run `wraith-node pair <relay-url> <ticketId>` over SSH → scan the QR printed in the terminal or paste the `WRP1-` code on the owner device
   4. `wraith-node service install` (launchd LaunchAgent on macOS, systemd user unit on Linux). Homebrew installs use this command, not `brew services` (one mechanism for every channel). **After a reboot with nobody logged in:** on Linux the user unit starts only with linger on, so `service install` runs `loginctl enable-linger $USER` (or prints the command when that needs sudo) and `doctor` checks `Linger=yes`; on macOS a LaunchAgent starts only after the user logs in, so a headless Mac needs automatic login (not possible with FileVault on) or someone logging in once after each reboot; `doctor` says which applies (Q14)
   5. **Local mode on a headless machine** works only through an SSH tunnel (`ssh -L 7777:127.0.0.1:7777 <host>`, then open the setup link on the computer at the other end). The local port must be 7777, because `allowedOrigins` is `http://localhost:7777` (§11.1), so it cannot be used while a Wraith node already runs on that computer. Documented in `docs/self-host.md`, not a main path

### 3.5 Hosting: who pays, who deploys

| Option | Use when | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Local** (default) | Everyone, to start immediately | No cost, no account |
| **Self-hosted / user's own Cloudflare** | Multiple machines, phone, watch | Cloudflare: the Workers Free plan supports SQLite-backed DOs ✅, quota is enough for one user (§3.6) |
| ~~Our team's public relay~~ | – | **Not doing this** (DECISION-7) |
| **Demo site** (static replay) | Judges | Does not connect to a real relay (§15) |

### 3.6 Cloudflare quotas (Workers Free) ✅ and the designed budget

| Quota (per day, per account) | Value ✅ | Wraith usage 💡 |
|---|---|---|
| Requests | 100,000 | Inbound WS messages **count 20:1** ✅. Outbound messages and protocol pings are not counted ✅ · Each alarm counts as a request · The watch HTTP API (long-poll) counts 1 request per call → limit polling to ≤ 1 per 25s per watch |
| Duration | 13,000 GB-s | **Hibernation API** ✅ (`ctx.acceptWebSocket`) · Liveness uses **WS auto-response** (`setWebSocketAutoResponse`), which answers app-level pings without waking the DO 🧪 Q22 (protocol-level ping/pong does not wake the DO ✅) · No app-level heartbeat that wakes the DO, and no frequent alarms |
| Rows written | 100,000 | **Do not store deltas or console frames.** Store only completed events, about 20–60 rows per prompt · `setAlarm()` counts as 1 row write ✅ · A DO has a single alarm slot, so keep a `timers` table and set the alarm to the nearest time |
| Rows read | 5,000,000 | Replay by `lastSeq` and snapshot |
| Storage | 5 GB total | Keep events for 30 days |

- If a quota is exceeded, operations error ✅ → the node enters local-only mode (sprites keep working, output is buffered) and the PWA shows an `E_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` banner (§13)
- Limits: `serializeAttachment` per WS is at most 16,384 bytes ✅ (store only `memberId`, `kind`) · The maximum WS message size must be checked 🧪 Q20 (relay-core enforces its own default limits of **256 KB per envelope** and **64 KB** per console frame, adjustable from the Q20 result)

---

## 4. Component Design

### 4.1 Relay adapter: HTTP layer (Cloudflare = Worker, Node = Fastify)

| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `assets` | Serves the PWA build (Cloudflare: Workers static assets · Node: static files) · Sends header `X-Wraith-Build` = build hash (shown in the UI, §12 boundary 3) |
| `ws-gate` | `GET /ws?space=…` → sends a challenge (128-bit nonce, valid 60s) → client replies with a signature from a key in the roster → hands the WebSocket to relay-core with `memberId` · Checks `Origin` for devices (allowlist from config §11.2) |
| `bootstrap` | `POST /space` creates the Space; requires `SETUP_TOKEN` + per-IP rate limit (5/min) · Allowed once per relay |
| `pair` | `GET /pair?ticket=…` lets a not-yet-paired member connect temporarily, only with a ticket created by the owner (10 minutes, single use) + rate limit |
| `api` | `/api/v1/*` HTTP API for watches and clients without WS (§6.7); verifies the request signature before passing it to relay-core |
| `health` | `GET /healthz` → `{ok, version, mode, spaceCreated}`; no auth; does not reveal spaceId |

### 4.2 relay-core (runs in the Space DO or in a Node process)

> This table describes the Cloudflare mode, which is the hardest · In the Node adapter: `Sockets` holds WS in memory, presence uses plain ping/pong, `Timers` uses `setTimeout`, `Storage` uses `better-sqlite3` (same schema, §10.1) · Lease/presence logic is written to stay correct even if in-memory state is lost, so it works in both modes

| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `hub` | Accepts WS with **tag = memberId**, attachment = `{memberId, kind}`, forwards envelopes by `meta.to`, and batches outgoing messages every 50–100ms |
| `sequencer` | Assigns `seq` into `relay.seq` (outside the signature scope), then writes to `events` before forwarding · Duplicate `meta.id` → no new write; replies `ack{id, original seq}` · `persist:false` (delta, console frame) gets no `seq` and is not replayed |
| `queue` | Stores **`persist:true`** envelopes addressed to an offline node in `outbox_relay` (a `persist:false` D→N request such as `media.get`, `fs.read`, `ws.diff` to an offline node is answered at once with `E_BOT_OFFLINE`, C74) and delivers them when the node returns (`hello{lastAckedId}`) · Keeps them for at most **7 days**, then drops them with an `E_QUEUE_DROPPED` card |
| `presence` | **Keeps no state in memory** · node/device sends `ping` every 15s → the runtime replies `pong` itself (auto-response) 🧪 Q22 · **Status is computed on read:** `lastSeen = max(autoResponseTimestamp of the socket with the matching tag if still open, members.connect_ts, members.last_seen)` · **Always record `members.last_seen` in `webSocketClose`/`webSocketError`** · If the lease holder has neither a socket nor `last_seen`, use `space.boot_ts`; never use "now" · Online when the socket is open and `now − lastSeen < 45s` · Socket closed = **start counting**, not immediate death |
| `roster` | Stores the owner-signed list and verifies the signature on receipt (the relay verifies to block junk, but nodes always verify too) · Checks the **cap** for every command whose route ends at the relay (`room.freeze`, `room.create/rename`, `room.members`, `crypt.list`, `console.open/close`, `push.subscribe/unsubscribe`, pairing, the watch HTTP API) and, as an early filter, for commands routed to a node (the node stays authoritative, SI-3) |
| `router` | Routes from `meta` only: `meta.mentions[]`, `meta.prompt.to`, `meta.to` · Does not need to read the body |
| `loop-guard` | Counts forward hops only (≤ 4) + total budget per root (≤ 12) + ≤ 6 forwards/min/room + Freeze room · **The relay computes `root` and `forwardDepth` itself from the `prompts` table via `replyTo`** · Prompts from `pane-unverified` have a separate budget (≤ 3 forwards/min) |
| `lease-authority` | Grants leases with a fencing token (`lease_epoch`), all stored in SQLite · Declares a holder dead when its socket closes or its auto-response timestamp is older than 45s · A single alarm is set to the next check time (§7.7) |
| `cloud-host` | Picks the node that runs the sprite of a cloud bot (§7.4.2) |
| `approvals` | **Forwards only; does not decide** · Stores only meta (`approvalId`, `bot`, `epoch`, `status`, `expiresAt`, `request_hash`) for display, push, and the watch inbox · `approval.request`/`approval.resolved` are **signed by the node** · a request with `autopilot:true` gets no push and no watch-inbox entry (§7.2.1) |
| `push` | Web Push (VAPID, RFC 8291) 🧪 Q19 · Works in every mode that has internet access |
| `crypt-index` | Merges session refs reported by nodes, then removes those that hold a lease |
| `api` | Logic for `/api/v1/*` (inbox snapshot, long-poll via `Timers`, roster export, cmd ingest) (§6.7) |
| `retention` | Deletes events > 30 days, API `seen` nonces > 10 minutes, expired pairing tickets (via `timers`, once a day) |

### 4.3 wraith-node (every machine, one codebase)

| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `relay-link` | Outbound WSS + challenge-signature + reconnect (backoff 1s→30s) · `hello{kind:"node", nodeId, herdrVersion (the herdr-core version), lastAckedId, lastSeq, bots[], leasesHeld[]}` · Sends `ping` every 15s and records **the send time of each ping** (FIFO) → `freshAt` = send time of the latest ping that got a pong (monotonic clock that counts sleep, §7.4.2) · **On-disk outbox** (`~/.wraith/node.db`) for events the relay has not acked yet (max 50MB) · In Local mode, uses an in-process channel instead of WS, behind the same interface (`RelayLink`) |
| `relay-embedded` | (Local mode) Creates relay-core + Node adapter in the same process as the node, binds `127.0.0.1:7777`, and serves the PWA |
| `verifier` | Verifies signed-command signatures against the roster that is **signed by the owner, with the owner key pinned by the node** · Rejects a roster with a version lower than the pinned one · Revocation is permanent · Blocks replay with **`meta.id` in `seen_cmds`** (8 days) **and** rejects `ts` older than 7 days (clock skew 5 minutes) · Per-type conditions (§6.2) · Checks the signer's **cap** against the command type (§12.2) · **The node is the dedupe point for prompts** |
| `herdr-client` | JSON-RPC 2.0, one message per line, over the hc socket ✅ (`node.json` `herdr.socket` if set, else hc's own order ✅: `$HC_SOCKET`, the `socket` setting in `~/.config/herdr-core/config.toml`, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/herdr-core/<session>.sock`, `/run/user/<uid>/herdr-core/<session>.sock` if that directory exists, else `<state>/<session>/<session>.sock` with `<state>` = `$XDG_STATE_HOME/herdr-core` or `~/.local/state/herdr-core`; `doctor` prints the path it used and Q2 checks it against hc; Unix socket paths must stay under the OS limit, 103 bytes on macOS ✅) · socket missing → retry every 5 s (`E_HERDR_UNAVAILABLE`; `hc server start` only at node start, §7.6) · at connect: `hello{generation:1}` (`unsupported_generation` → `E_HERDR_UNAVAILABLE` "update herdr-core"); the reply's `methods` must contain every method Wraith uses, and `core.id` is stored with each pane (§7.6) · one connection for calls and one for `events.subscribe{labels:{"wraith.node":<nodeId>}}` (snapshot, then ordered `created`/`lost`/`restarted`/`finished`/`removed`/`status`/`labels` events and a `heartbeat` every 15 s; no heartbeat for 45 s → reconnect) · on `missed` the node rebuilds its view from `missed.params.terminals` and drops events with `seq` ≤ `missed.params.seq` ✅; and `seq` starts again with each core process, so a reconnect always starts from a new snapshot ✅ · a `labels` event that drops a pane out of the filter is checked with `terminal.get` on the stored address (§7.6) · **Uses only** `hello`, `server.status`, `terminal.run/list/get/read/send/keys/kill/rm` and `events.subscribe` (+ `terminal.attach{observe}` from P9.5); **never** `machine.*`, `file.*`, `machine.exec`, `doc.*`, `templates.list`, `terminal.put`, `terminal.wait` or the `on`/`parent` params (DECISION-58) |
| `control` | Serves `~/.wraith/control.sock` (C72): `pair`, `unpair`, `status` from the same OS user only; checks the Switch rule (§3.4 item 7) and answers `busy` or performs the switch; never reachable from the relay, `/local/v1/ws` or the network |
| `pane-manager` | Starts a pane for each agent session (main + seats; `swap` mode: one pane per bot, DECISION-54) from the bot's agent + instructions (§4.8) with `terminal.run{argv:[<absolute path of wraith-sprite> (`<bin-dir>` rule, §4.6.1), "--bot", <botId>, …ids only], cwd:<workspace realpath>, env:{the login environment captured once at node start with `<shell> -l -c "printf '\0WRAITH_ENV\0'; env -0"` (`<shell>` = `$SHELL`, else the user's passwd entry; NUL-separated so multi-line values survive, parsed only after the marker so profile output is ignored; killed after 10 s → fall back to the core's environment with a doctor warning; values are never logged), filtered to `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`/`LC_*` and the variables the agent's launch spec names, + `WRAITH_*`}, labels:{"wraith.node":<nodeId>, "wraith.bot":<botId>, "wraith.session":"main"\|<roomId>, "wraith.spawn":<spawnToken>}, no_parent:true, no_notify:true, outlive_parent:true, keep:false}` ✅ Q2 (argv[0] is always an absolute path, so it can never name an hc template; a pane gets the core's environment plus these ✅; a core started by launchd or the Herdr Core app is expected to have a minimal one 🧪 Q2, hence the captured login environment) · records the returned address in `bot_sessions.pane_id` · respawns panes after a loss (§7.6) · closing a bot: `terminal.kill`, then `terminal.rm` · the sprite removes `HC_*` (`HC_TERMINAL_ID`, `HC_SESSION`, `HC_SOCKET`), any `HERDR_*` and `WRAITH_SPAWN` from the env of the agent under it |
| `sprite-hub` | Unix socket `~/.wraith/sprite.sock` (0600) accepts sprites, with ack by event `id` (§6.5) · one live connection per `(botId, sessionId)` (swap: per bot); a second `hello` gets `E_SPRITE_DUPLICATE` (§7.6) |
| `mcp-endpoint` | MCP over Streamable HTTP on `127.0.0.1:<port>`, with a per-session token in a header · Converts tool calls into envelopes with full meta, then sends them via relay-link (§6.4) |
| `media` | (P9.19, DECISION-57, C74) Serves `wraith_share_file` and device uploads (code in `apps/node/src/media/`) · **File check:** realpath must be inside the bot's own workspace; the §4.9 deny-list is matched on the path **relative to the workspace root** (so a standalone bot in `~/.wraith/scratch/<name>/` or an assistant folder is not denied by its own location), without §4.9's binary and git-ignored rules, and `.wraith/` inside the workspace is denied except `.wraith/media/` · open once with `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat` (regular file, size), then copy through that fd into `~/.wraith/media/cache/<mediaId>`; the hash, thumbnail, preview and streaming use only that copy (no check-then-reopen) · type from magic bytes in the allowlist `image/png\|jpeg\|webp\|gif`, `video/mp4\|webm\|quicktime` (no SVG, no HEIC from bots) · **Images:** metadata is removed by a deterministic TypeScript stripper (JPEG APP1/APP13/COM, PNG `tEXt`/`iTXt`/`zTXt`/`eXIf` such as ComfyUI workflows, WebP `EXIF`/`XMP`, GIF comment and application extensions except NETSCAPE looping) — no re-encode, so the stripped bytes and their SHA-256 are the same every time; `full` serves that stripped copy · **Video:** `ffmpeg` (child process, argv array, no shell, one job at a time, 120 s kill) makes the poster and a 720p H.264 MP4 preview with `-map_metadata -1`; the original never leaves the machine; when the preview would exceed `videoPreviewMaxMB` the card is still emitted with `preview:false` · thumbnail ≤ 32 KB WebP (longest side 480 px; JPEG when ffmpeg has no `libwebp`) · uploads go to `<workspace>/.wraith/media/in/<mediaId>-<name>` after the §4.8 `.wraith/` exclude step has run, opened with `O_CREAT\|O_EXCL` after a realpath check that the final path is inside that folder · **Limits:** ≤ 1 active transfer per device and ≤ 4 per node (`E_RATE_LIMITED`, `retryAfter`); the cache keeps stripped images and previews, LRU by last `media.get` within `cacheGB` · `doctor` reports `ffmpeg`, an H.264 encoder (`h264_videotoolbox`/`libx264`/`libopenh264`) and `libwebp`; without them cards have no thumbnail and video shows "Install ffmpeg with H.264 to preview" |
| `hook-endpoint` | For T2 and as the **fallback for T1-Kiro** (§7.2): `POST /hook` + header `X-Wraith-Bot-Token`, and stores a **one-shot grant** keyed by `(botId, sessionId, epoch, hash(tool,args,cwd))` → TTL 5 minutes; consuming a grant is audited · **Terminal mode** (DECISION-52): `POST /hook/terminal {event:"prompt"\|"stop"\|"end", sessionId}` with the same header, from the guard's `UserPromptSubmit`/`Stop`/`SessionEnd` hooks; it only reports busy/idle/exit and can never answer a permission |
| `context-builder` | Builds `context` (room summary ≤ 30 lines) from the history the node has (only final texts: agent thinking, tool commentary and deltas never enter any shared context, DECISION-53) · **Allowlist (SI-6):** `user.message` (device-signed, or labelled `pane-unverified`), `bot.message.done` (never `bot.terminalTurn`: turns imported from Terminal mode are shown in the chat only, DECISION-52), `bot.post`, `room.handoff`/`room.return`, each with a valid signature · `media.*` never enters context: a bot sees uploads only as the `[Attached image: …]` lines in its own prompt (C74) · **never** `system.card`, `bot.worked_for`, `approval.*`, `bot.tool`, `prompt.*`, `bot.summary` (summaries enter only as the labelled block of §4.8) · `wraith_room_context` uses the same allowlist |
| `redactor` | Scans every body (including tool output and diffs) with a secret scanner **before it leaves the machine** · Same pattern set as `tools/redact.ts` · fields declared `binary` in the protocol schema (`thumb`, `data`, `audio`) are skipped; their text siblings (`name`, `caption`) are scanned (C74) |
| `console-stream` | §8 |
| `crypt-scanner` | Every 60s and immediately when a bot closes; then reports `crypt.snapshot` (P8) |
| `herdr-guard` | Would audit input typed into a sprite's pane by another hc client 🧪 Q12 · herdr-core 0.1.16 has no event naming who sent input ✅, so this module is **expected to be dropped** (limitation recorded in §12) |
| `clock` | Small native helper for a monotonic clock that counts sleep (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME` / `mach_continuous_time`) 🧪 · If unavailable, **disable the `cloud-host` cap on that machine** |

### 4.4 wraith-sprite (universal T1 adapter)

```
herdr pane
└─ wraith-sprite --bot <botId> [--seat <roomId>] [--resume <sessionId>] [--lease-epoch <n>]
     ├─ ask sprite-hub for the bot's launch spec (agent from agents.json + instructions) → argv e.g. kiro-cli acp --agent wraith-<botId> --agent-engine v3   (if Q43 shows V3 rejects --agent: no --agent; the sprite selects wraith-<botId> after initialize via session/set_mode or the agent config option)
     ├─ spawn agent (stdio JSON-RPC, ACP) ✅  · env: remove HC_*, HERDR_* and WRAITH_SPAWN
     ├─ renderer → pane stdout (readable, append-only, no complex redraw)
     ├─ ACP client impl:
     │    sessionUpdate     → event (ULID id) → ring buffer → sprite-hub
     │    requestPermission → approval.request{approvalId=ULID, epoch} → sprite-hub  (§7.2)
     │    createTerminal    → 🧪 Q4 ask the node to open a herdr pane alongside and run the command there
     │    read/writeTextFile→ local fs (not a security control)
     ├─ prompt queue (owned by the sprite) → session/prompt one at a time, emits prompt.queued / prompt.started
     ├─ OSC 7501 status on its own stdout (state + app=wraith + title), shown by hc and Herdr.app (§9) ✅
     ├─ outgoing ring buffer of 2,000 events (consecutive deltas merged into one chunk), replayed when sprite-hub reconnects
     │    if the buffer overflows → send sprite snapshot = { text of the current turn, open approvals, queue, state }
     └─ refuse to spawn if argv has --trust-all-tools / --trust-tools=* (checked at runtime) (SI-2)
```

**Sprite input modes (pane stdin)** 💡

| Mode | How to enter | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `prompt` (default) | – | Readline on the bottom line; Enter puts it into the same prompt queue as the phone and emits `user.message{by:"pane-unverified"}` (no signature, §12); counted in a separate loop-guard budget |
| `command` | `/` prefix | `/cancel`, `/queue`, `/status`, `/detach`, `/exit` |
| `approve` | **Disabled** ✅ DECISION-2 | Approval only via commands signed by a device |
| *group seat* (`--seat`) | – | **No input at all** (DECISION-34): the sprite ignores pane stdin and shows a fixed footer "Group seat for # <room> · read-only · talk in the group". Messages to a group come only from the group chat. Main-session panes keep `prompt`/`command` modes (typed text → the bot's own chat, labelled `pane-unverified`) · **swap mode** (DECISION-54): there is no seat pane; the bot's one pane shows a `# <group>` chip while it serves a seat and keeps `prompt`/`command` modes, whose text always goes to the bot's own chat |

**Sprite modes:** `--mode acp` (default) · `--mode sdk` (Claude Code through the **Claude Agent SDK**, DECISION-47, mapping table below: the sprite maps SDK messages to the same events as ACP. **Approval gate = an in-process SDK `PreToolUse` hook** registered by the sprite, which runs before the SDK's deny/mode/allow rules and before `canUseTool`: every non-read tool call creates an `approval.request` and is allowed only after a verified `approval.respond` (or a valid Autopilot decision, §7.2.1); `canUseTool` stays as a second gate with the same rule. Settings allow-rules alone can never let a write/execute run, because the hook runs first 🧪 Q36. The hook is registered with `timeout = approvalTimeoutMin × 60 + 60` s 🧪; it returns `permissionDecision:"deny"` on reject, timeout or any error, so a settings-file hook returning `allow` cannot win; `canUseTool` joins the same pending `approvalId` instead of raising a second request. If Q36 shows the SDK cannot hold a long hook, the hook denies immediately and the sprite re-prompts "approved, retry" after the answer (like the Kiro hook path, §7.2). `settingSources` is always empty (no `user`, `project` or `local` source, DECISION-60) 🧪; whether the project's instructions file still reaches the bot with no `project` source is checked in spike task 13.8 and, if it does not, becomes an open item. The permission mode is always an asking mode (`manual`, or `default` if the SDK types still name it so 🧪 13.8), never any other value (the unsafe set lives in `packages/protocol/src/unsafe.ts`), and the prompts option, if the SDK has one, is `host`; `allow_always` lives only in the sprite's memory for the current epoch and is never returned as `updatedPermissions` (so nothing is written to Claude settings) (SI-2); `enableFileCheckpointing: true` and `extraArgs['replay-user-messages']` are always on so every prompt has a checkpoint uuid) · `--mode exec` (Cloud Status Bot §7.4-3: each prompt spawns `kiro-cli chat --no-interactive --resume-id <id> "<task>"`)

**Terminal mode** (DECISION-52, §8.1): on `terminal.enter` the sprite closes its agent child (ACP or SDK), waits for it to exit, then spawns the agent's **own TUI as a child in the same pane** with the TTY handed over (the sprite stays the pane process, so the pane, the lease and the exit code stay with it); while the TUI runs the sprite reads no pane input, keeps its ring buffer and hub socket, and reports `terminal.state`; when the TUI exits it reports `terminal.return` and stays in `exiting`; it resumes the same session with the normal driver only on `N→G terminal.resume` (sent by the node after the return path and its SI-2 re-check passed), or exits on `bot.shutdown`. Group seat sprites (`--seat`) and `--mode exec` never enter Terminal mode.

**Claude SDK ↔ sprite mapping (`--mode sdk`, DECISION-47; every 🧪 item is checked in Q36):**

| Sprite concept (ACP name) | Claude Agent SDK |
|---|---|
| start session (`session/new`) | `query()` with streaming input, `cwd` = workspace, `systemPrompt: {type:"preset", preset:"claude_code", append: <Wraith context + instructions>}` 🧪 |
| resume (`session/load`) | `resume: <sessionId>` 🧪 |
| prompt (`session/prompt`) | next user message on the streaming input |
| cancel (`session/cancel`) | `interrupt()` 🧪 |
| permission (`session/request_permission`) | `PreToolUse` hook (gate) + `canUseTool` (second gate) 🧪 |
| tool/message updates (`session/update`) | SDK assistant/user/tool messages mapped to `bot.message.delta/done`, `bot.tool` |
| commands (`available_commands_update`) | `supportedCommands()` 🧪 Q35 |
| fork (`session/fork`) | `resume` + `forkSession: true` (Crypt Fork) 🧪 |
| rewind points | user-message `uuid` (with `replay-user-messages`) → `G→N rewindPoint` |
| rewind code / conversation | `rewindFiles(uuid, {dryRun})` 🧪 / resume with `resumeSessionAt: uuid` 🧪 |
| instructions from the workspace | the SDK loads `CLAUDE.md` and settings from `user` + `project` sources; settings are checked before launch (SI-2) |

**Lifecycle:**
- **agent crash** (exit ≠ 0): restart with backoff, bump epoch, `session/load` ✅; after 3 times within 10 minutes → `failed` (the sprite reports it and stays in its pane; the node records intent `failed`, §7.6)
- **sprite or pane ends:** decided by the node's close intent (§7.6), never by the exit code alone: the sprite exits 0 only after the node acked its `closing`, and 75 on signals or a hung-up TTY · a recorded `close`, or the pane `killed` by a person (Herdr.app close, `hc kill`): no respawn; release the lease, and the session goes to the Crypt · `pause`/`moved`: no respawn, not the Crypt · `failed`: no respawn, lease released, Resurrect from the Crypt · anything else (crash, reboot, holder died): the node respawns the sprite, with backoff; a 4th respawn within 10 minutes → `failed` (it must still hold the lease)
- **herdr-core restart or update:** programs keep running in their holders ✅; the node reconnects and reconciles from the snapshot (§7.6)
- **relay unreachable:** the sprite keeps working in **local-only** · local bot: lease `offline-held` (§7.7) · cloud bot: **stop sending new work to Kiro as soon as `now − freshAt ≥ 35s`** (self-fencing), and if it gets `lease.lost` on reconnect, the sprite exits (§7.4.2)

**ACP engine: v3 required for every bot** ✅ DECISION-3 (`session/list` and remote sessions exist only in V3 ✅ · the default for `kiro-cli acp` is v2 ✅) · Use `--agent-engine v3` per process or `kiro-cli settings acp.agentEngine v3` ✅ · The sprite checks `agentCapabilities.loadSession` ✅ from `initialize` before calling `session/load`; if `sessionCapabilities.resume` ✅ exists (ACP `session/resume` = restore without replay), use it as the alternative in Q5b

---
### 4.5 PWA (React + Vite + Tailwind)

| View | Content |
|---|---|
| **Room timeline** | Bubbles (streaming), tool cards, diffs, handoff/return cards, T0 status cards (**labeled "raw snapshot"**), PR cards, system cards (respawned, host moved, queue dropped) |
| **Bot rail** | Avatar + state + machine badge (`Mac`, `V100`, `Kiro·Cloud via V100`) + Tier + number of queued prompts |
| **Approval sheet** | Title, kind, cwd, command or diff, buttons from `options[].kind`, countdown to `expiresAt` · **Verifies the node signature on the request before display** (fails = no Allow button) · On tap, **signs with the device key**, bound to `requestHash` |
| **Console drawer** | xterm.js read-only + Take control (§8) |
| **Crypt** | Dormant list grouped by machine and project; Resurrect / Fork (Claude) buttons disabled during the restore grace window (P8) |
| **Machines & Devices** | Grouped by computer with role chips (machine / app / browser / phone / watch, access Full / Approve only / View only, owner); pause or revoke per role; cloud-host lease holder and Move host; Add machine / Add device (§3.4, §14.1 `/machines`) |
| **Settings** | As in §14.1 `/settings` (DECISION-40) |
| **Key storage** | Device key is a non-extractable WebCrypto `CryptoKey` in IndexedDB, **one per Space** (keyed by `spaceId`; the active Space's key signs) · If storage is cleared, the device must pair again |
| **Transport** | `WsTransport` (real) · `DemoTransport` (replays fixtures, §15) · same interface |

Screen, state, and copy details are in §14

### 4.5.1 Host capability layer 💡 DECISION-30

One PWA build runs everywhere. At start it detects its **host** once and every feature asks the layer instead of branching in screens (`packages/ui-kit` exports `host` and `can(feature)`):

| Host | How it is detected |
|---|---|
| `tauri` | `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__` present (desktop app) |
| `browser-local` | served from `http://localhost:7777` / `127.0.0.1` by the node of this machine (Local mode) |
| `browser-remote` | any other origin in a desktop browser (relay URL) |
| `mobile` | `browser-remote` + touch/standalone display (installed PWA on a phone) |

| Feature (`can(...)`) | tauri | browser-local | browser-remote | mobile | Fallback when missing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `knowThisMachine` (preselect "This Mac") | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | user picks the machine |
| `nativeFolderPicker` (real absolute path) | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | `fs.list` browser + "Add folder by path" |
| `dropFolderPath` (Finder drag & drop) | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | hidden |
| `localApprovalChannel` (127.0.0.1 when the relay is down, DECISION-22) | ✅ | n/a (relay is local) | ❌ | ❌ | approvals wait for the relay |
| `tray`, `globalShortcut`, `autostart`, `sidecar` | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | hidden |
| notifications | native | Web Notification | Web Notification / Web Push | Web Push (installed PWA, iOS ≥ 16.4) | in-app badge (DECISION-8) |
| device key storage | WebCrypto or OS keychain (Q24a) | WebCrypto (IndexedDB, non-extractable) | same | same | – |

Rules: never call a Tauri API without `can(...)`; a feature missing on a host is **hidden or replaced by its fallback**, never shown disabled without a reason; the CI E2E suite runs the PWA as `browser-local`, `browser-remote` and `mobile` (viewport + touch), and the Tauri suite covers `tauri` (§21.3).

### 4.6 Desktop app (Tauri v2) 💡 ✅ DECISION-12

```
Wraith.app (Tauri v2)
├─ WebView (WKWebView / WebView2 / WebKitGTK) → loads the same PWA build (origin tauri://localhost or http://localhost:7777 per Q24a)
├─ Rust shell: tray/menu bar, native notifications, global shortcut, auto-start, single-instance, updater
├─ bundled CLI: wraith-sprite (linked onto PATH at first run, so it can be run by hand; the node starts it by absolute path)
└─ sidecar: wraith-node (single file: Node SEA + prebuilt better-sqlite3 by default · bun --compile if Q24b passes)
     └─ relay-core Local mode on 127.0.0.1:7777 + herdr ⇄ sprites
```

| Capability | Details |
|---|---|
| **Manage `wraith-node`** | Starts the sidecar when the app opens (or at login if auto-start is on) and shuts it down gracefully on quit (SIGTERM → wait 5s → SIGKILL) · If a `wraith-node` is already running (e.g. from launchd), connect to it instead (check `GET http://127.0.0.1:7777/healthz`) |
| **Sidecar config** ✅ | `bundle.externalBin: ["binaries/wraith-node"]` and a file named `wraith-node-<target-triple>` (e.g. `wraith-node-aarch64-apple-darwin`) · Spawned from Rust with `app.shell().sidecar("wraith-node")` · Capability `shell:allow-spawn` + `shell:allow-execute` with `sidecar: true` and an args allowlist |
| **Menu bar / tray** | Sprite icon + badge with the number of pending approvals · Menu §14.3 |
| **Native notifications** | approval, ask_human, bot error, host moved · Clicking opens the matching approval sheet directly · On desktop this replaces Web Push |
| **Global shortcut** | Default `⌥⌘W` (macOS) / `Ctrl+Alt+W`; opens the Wraith window at the latest approval |
| **Remote mode** | Can be configured to connect to another relay (Self-hosted/Cloudflare) instead of Local (the desktop is a regular device, and the sidecar node pairs with that relay) |
| **Direct local channel** 💡 (DECISION-22) | In remote mode the desktop also connects straight to the **node on the same machine** at `ws://127.0.0.1:7777/local/v1/ws` (always on, bound to 127.0.0.1 only, `Origin` must be the app's own origin). Auth = the same ws-auth challenge (§6.3) with the desktop's device key. Over it the node sends the open `approval.request`s of **its own bots**, and the desktop sends signed `approval.respond` (and `room.freeze`). The node runs the **same verifier** (roster, pinned owner key, cap, `seen_cmds`, `requestHash`), so SI-1/SI-3/SI-5 are unchanged. Whichever copy of a command arrives first wins; the other is dropped as `E_REPLAY`. The node forwards `approval.resolved` to the relay through its outbox when the link returns, so phone/watch catch up. Scope: approvals and freeze for bots **on this machine only** (Freeze also turns Autopilot off, so a runaway Autopilot can be stopped with the relay down); everything else still goes through the relay. Not a new trust path: a process without the device key cannot sign |
| **Command signing** | WebCrypto in the WebView, same as the PWA 🧪 Q24a · If that fails, move the device key to the OS keychain via a Tauri command (Rust signs ECDSA) |

**Distribution (3 levels)** ✅ DECISION-13

| Level | Method | What the user must do | Requires Apple Developer ID |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Build from source** (current default) | Clone the repo and build it yourself | Opens immediately (ad-hoc signed on Apple Silicon) | No |
| **2. Unsigned release** | GitHub Actions builds `.dmg` (macOS universal) + AppImage/.deb (Linux), attached to GitHub Releases on every `v*` tag | macOS: "Open Anyway" or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/Wraith.app` · Linux: works immediately | No |
| **3. Signed + notarized** (later) | Same workflow + Developer ID secrets + notarytool | Double-click to open | Yes ($99/year) |

```bash
# Requires: Xcode Command Line Tools, Rust (rustup), Node 22 + pnpm
git clone https://github.com/<owner>/wraith && cd wraith
pnpm install
pnpm --filter @wraith/desktop tauri build
# Produces Wraith.app and .dmg in apps/desktop/src-tauri/target/release/bundle/
```

**GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml`):** triggered by tag `v*` · matrix `macos-latest` (universal) + `ubuntu-22.04` · builds the sidecar per target (universal = build both `aarch64-apple-darwin` and `x86_64-apple-darwin`, then `lipo` 🧪 Q24b) → `tauri-apps/tauri-action` creates a draft release · the same workflow builds the node tarballs (§4.6.1) and opens a pull request on the tap repo · Level 3 adds `APPLE_*` secrets · Auto-update requires the Tauri updater key as a secret

**Fallback:** Electron (can run `wraith-node` directly in the main process, at the cost of 100MB+ size) · Switch only if Q24a **and** the Q24b fallback both fail (§22.3)

#### 4.6.1 Install channels 💡 DECISION-59 (C76)

| Channel | Installs | Targets | Phase | Signing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GitHub Releases: `Wraith-<v>-universal.dmg`, AppImage, `.deb` | Wraith.app (bundles `wraith-node` + `wraith-sprite`) | macOS universal · Linux x86_64 | P5 | level 2 (unsigned) → level 3 in P9.9 |
| GitHub Releases: `wraith-node-<v>-<target>.tar.gz` + `SHA256SUMS` | `bin/wraith-node`, `bin/wraith-sprite`, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY.md` (no app); the layout follows Q24b 🧪: single-file executables in `bin/`, or (fallback) a portable Node + bundles in `lib/` with small launcher scripts in `bin/`; either way `bin/` is the `bin-dir` | `darwin-arm64`, `darwin-x64`, `linux-x64` (the lowest glibc it runs on is measured in P5 and written in the release notes 🧪) | P5 | macOS binaries are ad-hoc signed in CI (`codesign --sign -`), which Apple Silicon needs to run a Node SEA binary ✅ |
| Homebrew **formula** `wraith-node` in the tap `github.com/<owner>/homebrew-wraith` → `brew install <owner>/wraith/wraith-node` | the tarball above for the machine's target | macOS + Linux (Homebrew on Linux) | P5 | none: Homebrew does not set the quarantine flag on formula downloads, so Gatekeeper does not prompt 🧪 (P5-D14 checks it on a clean Mac) |
| Homebrew **cask** `wraith` in the same tap → `brew install --cask <owner>/wraith/wraith` | the dmg above | macOS | **P9.9**, only once the app is signed and notarized; its details (Homebrew's rules for casks, how it coexists with the formula) are specified in the P9.9 spec 🧪 | level 3 |
| Docker `wraith-relay` | the relay | linux/amd64, linux/arm64 | P4 | – |
| winget / Scoop | Windows builds | Windows | P9.21 | Windows code signing (H12) |

- **Wraith.app next to a formula node** (P5): the app does not start its own sidecar while a node already answers `GET 127.0.0.1:7777/healthz`; it attaches to it (P5-D5), and its first run skips linking `wraith-sprite` when that command already resolves to another install (first-run says which) · **two installs, one `~/.wraith`:** migrations only go forward (§10.3), so a node that finds a `node.db` schema newer than its own exits with "~/.wraith was upgraded by a newer Wraith (<path of the other install>); update this one", and `doctor` lists every `wraith-node` it finds (PATH, the Homebrew `opt` link, Wraith.app)
- **The tap is a separate repo** owned by the owner. Formula templates live in this repo (`deploy/homebrew/wraith-node.rb.tmpl`, later `wraith.rb.tmpl`). The release workflow builds on a `v*` tag into a **draft** release; the tap job runs only on the `release: published` event (after the owner publishes the draft, so the asset URLs work), fills in version and SHA-256 and opens a pull request on the tap: on its `main` branch for a normal release, on a `test` branch for a prerelease tag (`v*-test`, `v*-rc*`).
